ParlaMint-ES-GA_2016-04-26-DSPG150.u35	Thank you, Mrs. Rodríguez-Vispo. The turn of parliamentary groups to set their position on the dictatorship and, if so, to defend the amendments maintained. For the Joint Parliamentary Group, Mrs Martínez.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2016-04-26-DSPG150.u36	Well, after the debate in motion and in committee of this bill, we must reaffirm what we have said in the amendment to the whole. This social economy bill doesn't respond to the expectations its title promises, limiting its content to the creation of the Galician Social Economy Council and marking general goals. Although the debate was open, it was impossible to reach an agreement, as the text continues to present great problems, so we keep the amendments that I am going to defend as a whole. The first problem appears already in the first article, which sets as the only object recognized by this law to regulate the entities that integrate Galicia's social economy. Our Amendment No. 1 adds the role of providing you with mechanisms to facilitate its constitution and expansion, recognizing its contribution to Galicia's social and economic development and the equitable distribution of wealth and the generation of social heritage. This amendment was not accepted; therefore, the law remains nothing but an objective, which is to regulate the entities that integrate Galician social economy. We maintain all the amendments that include, within the social economy, those that are dedicated to non-entrepreneurship social activities. It is already recognised at European level that there are social economy entities that are engaged in truly economic activities, and others that provide services to society and that are not really business or economic activity, but services to citizenship. We maintain Amendments Nos 9 to 13 on those entities that are part of the social economy. Among them we find it important that trade unions be part of. Trade unions are service providers. Despite the provisions of article 9.3 of the State Law 5/2011, of social economy, this bill excludes from the entities of the social economy of Galicia trade union and business organizations, so the catalogue of social economy entities will not count on the representation of the working class, although, in addition, trade unions also have among their members and between their membership workers and workers of social economy entities and, therefore, with a very important role in the working conditions of these entities. Law 17/2008, of institutional participation of Galicia's most representative trade union and business organizations, recognizes to these organizations its involvement in economic and social life. However, this social economy bill envisages its participation in the Galician Social Economy Council with a collective representation that eliminates the right of each business and trade union organization to have one member per organization. It doesn't make sense, since in Galicia there are three trade union organizations that are the most representative and have different criteria, that have different visions and that have different affiliations. And, therefore, the three positions that do not match in their entirety and in their conception of the social economy itself should be represented. We also bet on the participation of feminist organizations, because they are entities that watch for gender equality in all areas of society, included in the field of economics. And here's something very important, because we've been denounced and it's recognized – not even the People's Party denies this reality – that women are discriminated against in access to the world of work, in working conditions and wages that apply to us, and we're even discriminated against when we try to start a social economy activity. Therefore, the representation of feminist organizations is important. It is also incomprehensible that it is not included in the Galician Social Economy Council of representation by feminist organizations when the need for participation by the Directorate-General for Equality is admitted. So if there's that important recognition of women's rights, and for that, there's the Directorate-General for Equality, but not the organizations that represent women, social organizations. We also demand the participation of fair trade organizations, social credit institutions and NGOs, all with great influence on the economy. They all work for a fairer distribution of wealth, so they all have a fundamental role in the social economy. In particular, NGOs have a great deal of experience in the field of the social economy, and today they are responding to the actions that governments throughout the world should take on this matter. We maintain Amendment No 16, which adds a new chapter for the creation of the Galego Social Economy Institute, the executive body responsible for instrumenting public policies to promote the social economy. To the Council of the Social Economy of Galicia, whose creation seems to be the object of this bill, only optional and non-binding functions are granted, although its reports would represent the conclusions of all entities of the social economy of Galicia. So we keep Amendment Nos 17 and 18 so that their reports are binding. It doesn't make any sense to have a body where all the entities of the social economy are and that later their conclusions don't go anywhere, they have no binding character and they don't force the government to make any decision. But it is also more absurd that, as this council, a merely consultative council, without more functions, there is no executive instrument that leads forward, coordinates and acts to encourage the social economy. The draft law, as set out in Article 15, adds that the Eusumo Network is the instrument for promoting cooperativeism and the social economy, but we can see that its role is reduced to the area of cooperative work and labour societies. Such is the limitation of this bill and its lack of content which in its additional fourth provision states that this law will have no economic endowment for its development, which represents an irrefutable declaration of intent. There can be no law that can operate without financial resources. This is pure propaganda, that is, as it is usually said, a pamphlet by the People's Party, it's nothing more, it's nothing more than a pamphlet. Now, that's right, a pamphlet with very bad intentions, and I'll explain later why. But in principle it does not establish anything, no instrument, that really does give it as much as to worry about the maintenance of the social economy's activity. We therefore maintain Amendment No 34, which calls for the budgetary appropriations necessary to ensure at least the compensation of representation costs to social economy entities. Otherwise, the cost of establishing and operating the Council is transferred to non-profit entities, which, precisely because they are non-profit entities, should not have to bear more costs, the costs of operating a council that is created from the administration itself, but those who want to participate will have to have an budget to work, because it will not help in any way. So let's go back to that propaganda law. Let's keep Amendments 36 and 37 to the second transitional provision to ensure that all social services are subject to the prior authorisation regime. And here comes the really important thing of this law, which really matters to the People's Party, which is to put social services into the free market. Prior authorisation is terminated, entity control is terminated. This is going to be the big deal, and we're going to look at it now that we're starting to get into the debate of the Proposal for a Law amending the Social Services Act. You want to turn social services into the big business of private companies. A great risk we're going to run with all this. I have the only hope that they will have months of government left and that all these laws will have to change them, that they will have to be repealed and recast other laws that defend the interests of the Greek social majority, both in the Heritage Act, which we have just discussed, as in this Law of the Social Economy, and how we will very likely do with the Bill that amends the Law of Social Services. We keep the amendments to the last one, to 38, to limit the number of people involved in cooperatives in relation to the number of partners. It can't be that a cooperative can have more collaborators than partners; therefore, we want a limitation on the number of collaborators. And Amendment 39 is to establish that partner persons will not be able to develop economic activities that compete with the cooperative. Something that seems logical is not needed or explained that one cannot be economically supporting a cooperative and, on the other hand, having a business with economic interests to compete with this cooperative itself. And we keep Amendment 40 because we can't discriminate against the trial period of the people depending on the type of program they are hosting. There can't be six months for new people in a kind of social economy organization and initiatives and being more than six months away. However, we consider that six months of trial period is sufficient in any case for any worker, whether in the social economy or any worker who works for a non-profit company. We maintain all amendments to the last third provision amending Law 13/2008, which seeks to liberalize and lower the control of the Administration in social services. Moreover, the amendments to the Proposition of Law, as it said, of amendment to the Law on Social Services, are in time, so it makes no sense that we are prosecuting the amendment of a law that is at the same time being amended by another law, which in popular language is known as "a tangled one." What you're doing is a real mess with social services. What you're doing is you're turning them into a business and putting them into the hands of profit-oriented private companies precisely the most unprotected people, the most vulnerable people, the people who would need more support from the administration. So we will vote against this bill, because it does not only improve the social economy at all, it does not encourage social economy entities, it does not care about its consolidation within the productive fabric, it does not value its contribution to the country's economy, it does not value its contribution to a much more equitable distribution of wealth and, of course, it does not take into account any kind of women's difficulties in accessing jobs and creating their own social economy initiatives. But also because it doesn't recognize many social entities, such as fair trading entities or as trade unions, and changes important aspects of the Social Services Act to promote the provision of services by private entities with and without a profit. So, of course, what we're looking for in that modification -- and we'll see it with the modification of the Social Services Act -- is to convert rights into a big business, to charity rights. We will therefore vote against this bill. Nothing else, thank you very much.

But, on the other hand, we understand that this Social Economy Act will not respond to this series of problems we have in Galicia in both economic and employment. And I mean, the honourable Member of the People's Party already knows, our red line of the Socialist Group in this bill was located in social services. And we understand that the introduction of the whole articulation with regard to social services that is being done in this social economy bill that comes to us is to give free rein to some companies that should not have it. In the view of the Socialists, in times of crisis, entrepreneurial initiatives should be placed in two aspects: on the one hand, the creation of viable enterprises in emerging and job-generating sectors, and on the other hand, the consolidation, impulse and vertebration of traditional sectors. Nothing makes us think that this law will contribute to these solutions. The Social Economy Act presented by the Government of Galicia does not design indispensable instruments to address these measures to promote the social economy, nor does it globally address a competitive policy for the economy in Galicia. Many of these proposals are made on the subject of proposals which are in the law itself, and they do not even count on the sectoral reports of the relevant councils. For us this law shows a significant lack of funds, it does not include new financing measures for companies, it abounds in various utterances without real content and business effectiveness, requiring no real action measures in any of the areas which it intends to regulate the law. In substance, the legal text is reduced to the regulation and creation of the Social Economy Council, which is limited in other respects to setting out mere programmatic principles. So we understand that they do not give real solutions to the problems that I have just mentioned at the beginning of my intervention. The fact that the Social Economy Council does not have an budget makes it even more difficult to carry out another of its functions, which is the preparation of studies and proposals. On the other hand, one aspect which we consider, along with the other opposition groups and which we believe to be relevant, is the lack of representation of trade union centres. But again, I would like to point out that for the socialists the main problem is what is intended to be done: a reform of social services regulations. The law particularly affects providers of this type of service, and it really approves a reform of social services regulations and not with additional provisions. We therefore believe that the groups affected by the provision of services must have a share in this regard, because the groups directed at child care, the elderly, persons with disabilities, those involved in the fight against unemployment, training, vocational reintegration, the inclusion of persons, those targeted at social housing, among others, constitute a value far greater than the interest they intend to defend themselves in this text, which is the removal of requirements or obstacles to the development of activity. We do not agree on this, we say so at the meetings prior to the transaction agreements, we say so in the Commission itself. And we understand that these kinds of services should be subject to prior authorization. In our view, the reform of the social services legislation envisaged should have been carried out after the appropriate debate with the entities participating in the Galego de Wellness Council. Since, as I said before, these types of services are affected, in particular, by entities providing such services; therefore, a reform of social services regulations is being addressed, and, as I said, it cannot be done as it is presented in this law. We believe that those groups affected by the provision of services to very sensitive people cannot be removed from the requirements or obstacles to the development of the activity. Because it is a value that requires the relevant administrative check from the very first moment. From the very first moment, you have to be skilled workers, suitable facilities, etc.; therefore – I repeat – we understand that prior authorization should be subject to. We understand that in parallel with an initiative with regard to the social services that are being dealt with in Parliament, the Law of the Social Economy, which from the back aims to put the Greek social services at the height – with all due respect – of the opening of a hairdresser or a coffee shop, I believe that we are not acting in a responsible way and, above all, in a way that positively values our social services, and that we do not liberalize. I mean, this law that puts above the table is a reform by the back door of social services, a reprivatization of social services. So the socialists in this respect, I said, is the red line and we're not going through. And it is not worth, Mrs People's Party - which I said in the Commission - when it comes to Community directives, the Community directives are a part of this. They already make a difference for the treatment of social services and it's a much more demanding treatment than for the opening up of any other type of company or facility. The Socialists therefore announce the vote against. Thank you very much, Mr. President.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2016-04-26-DSPG150.u45	Thank you, Mrs. Quintas. People's Parliamentary Group. Mrs. Cancel.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2016-04-26-DSPG150.u46	Thank you, President. Counselor, ladies and gentlemen, good morning. First of all, I would also like to thank the other rapporteurs from the other groups for the good treatment we had at the various meetings of the Phoenician Parliament. Also to the Chamber's Juridical Services, to its lawyer, for their support in the work developed at the various meetings. From this group we made an effort to try to consensualize amendments by accepting some and trading others. I think the effort has been positive and the initial text has been improved among all of them. Nevertheless, we could not accept all the amendments tabled by the other groups, because some of them are directly related to the concept of the social economy. I am talking about those who call for the abolition of the term business economy or competitiveness, and those who ask for the inclusion in the catalogue of the social economy of entities that do not engage in business or do not have the appropriate legal formula – feminist associations, NGOs, trade unions, social credit institutions or fair trade –. Because it's not the social end that defines what is an entity of social economy, because in that case there would be still many more entities that perform the activity with a social purpose: associations of the sick, the Caritas and a long et cetera. That's why I'm going back to my intervention of the plenary when we discuss amendments to the whole of this law, don't worry, I won't talk again about those Catholic circles that in the 19th century sought to humanize the economy, understanding that it was overly focused on capital, but I'm going to talk about which were the first economists looking for a reorientation of the economy in which the real object is man and woman, and not wealth. I'm referring to the great influence of liberal economists John Stuart Mill and Leon Walras, who pointed out that the solution to the social problems of the time was not the suppression of capital, but to make the world less capitalist and more moral. They were looking for greater democracy in production processes, they were looking for regular hours of work for employees, who were suffering from abusive days. And pay particular attention to business associations among workers, advocating the promotion of cooperatives for their economic and moral advantages. This model will be developed in the next century in Western Europe, since in the countries of Central and Eastern Europe, linked to the Soviet system and with centralized planning economies, the State was the only economic actor, so there was no room for social economy agents. Because whether we stick to those early ideas of these 19th-century thinkers or if we look for the definitions that researchers make and institutions use in the 21st century, we're talking about companies in a different way to develop their economic activity. An activity based on people, internal democracy, solidarity, social cohesion, the inclusion of people at risk or social exclusion and even with different capacities. And all this, and more, is reflected in the guiding principles of this bill, because we are talking about a sector that does business economic activity, some for social purposes, but that they understand that social purposes are not contrary to these terms. In fact, the Galaga Business Association itself of Special Employment Centres without a spirit of Work uses these terms, uses the term entrepreneurship, the term competitiveness; which is not related to its object, which is the insertion of people with disabilities or with problems of job insertion. Because this is the spirit of the law: to value the entities of the social economy, to make their approaches to society, territory, especially in areas like that of the sea – we speak of fishermen's confrereries – the land – agricultural cooperatives, agricultural processing societies, or communities and communities of neighboring mountains – or work – labor societies, insertion companies or special employment centres. Nor can we support those amendments that remove entities from the catalogue because they are already recognised in the state law – speaking of mutualities or associations that carry out business activities – or changing their names by changing their meaning – leaving only confirmations and not specifying that they are fishermen. Because we cannot share the assessment that from the opposition groups is made to the bill, because it complies with the objectives that mark European institutions, the most advanced countries and the main researchers of the social economy, and that is the differentiated treatment that this sector deserves and that collects the law, contributing to the visibility and knowledge of it, providing it with a common legal framework that leaves it safe – as it cannot be otherwise, and therefore we cannot accept any other part of the amendments tabled – the peculiarities of the regulation of each of the categories of entities that compose it. In addition, it lays the foundations and principles on which future activity should be sustained. We also do not share those amendments concerning the reform of certain articles of the Portuguese Cooperatives Act, since they are improvements demanded by the sector, in line with the sector, and in which one of the great news of this law is gathered: the creation of the co-operative society of young people. Cooperatives that require minimum social capital for their constitution, which simplifies and undermines administrative procedures and that will be an important tool for promoting economic activities among young people, and therefore for improving their employability. Equal opportunities between men and women are also properly collected in the law. That's why we won't allow it to be said that this law does not collect equality. I will not allow it to be said that the Members of the People's Party of Galicia do not believe in equality. Furthermore, this law complies with all the recommendations of the preceptive gender impact report. In fact, the principle of equality between men and women is present throughout the text of the standard, in different articles, both in the guiding principles and in the composition of the Council of the Social Economy of Galicia or in the promotion and dissemination of the social economy. However, this group maintains the proposed transaction to Amendment No. 14 of the BNG, since we think it improves the text presented in this chamber. Another issue discussed in the Commission was the subject of specific financial instruments or budgetary allocation. We believe that a law must be a text that lasts over time, so to establish excessively concretely what financial instruments must be sealed to support social economy initiatives could leave out many opportunities, for example, the use of new European funds. The important thing is to collect in the text the need to establish that specific financial instruments are necessary depending on the type of entity. It's not the same as an internationalized cooperative, a project with a strong technological component, or the development of a small job insertion company or a special employment centre. The law takes these differences by establishing as possibilities, though open, the establishment of venture capital funds and specific microcredits for these companies. Because the important thing, as always, is how the collection is developed in the law and, therefore, this parliamentary group has presented itself in the amendment, which has already been incorporated into the motion, which establishes the obligation that the Government of Greece, within a maximum of six months, will approve an incentive plan for these entities, a plan which will be discussed in the Galego Council of the Social Economy and which will therefore be agreed and spoken directly to the sector; they are those who know what their needs are. This plan, or any plans that can be developed in the future, will, as it is logical, have a budget for its implementation, even if the law does not collect it. We also do not share the need for new structures in the Galician Union. I'm talking about amendments that propose the creation of a Galego Institute of Social Economy or the duplication of Igape, with the creation of the Galego Institute of Credit and Investment. It seems incredible that many of those who have always been questioning the existence of many autonomous bodies within the administration propose the creation of new, unnecessary structures, at economic cost to the Administration, rather than using the resources available for what are really necessary: to support the initiatives taking place in this sector on our land. Another criticism is the alleged lack of content or the role of the Galician Council of Social Economy. This Council will have a broad representation of the different sectors of society, but above all of the entities that make up the social economy. There is union representation, which we believe should not be extended because the characteristics of these entities, in which the worker participates in decision-making, should not be sufficiently given. It would also not be right for the proposal that is being tabled through the amendment that would give rise to the incorporation of many more representatives, which would lead to the disenchantment of the Council. In addition, this Council incorporates the municipal representation and the Greek universities, which is not collected in state law; therefore, it improves. This body, which many opposition groups minimize, is of vital importance, since it is the only organ in which it is recognised and voiced to the entire diversity of social economy entities in Galicia, increases the possibilities of non-institutional collaboration and will have an important role in the design of policies that have been established since the German Government. Because this law is a very relevant law, since we're talking about entities that provide stable and quality jobs. We are talking about visibility, encouragement, financial support of these types of entities. Because this law also incorporates as a novelty the inclusion of social clauses that favour the participation of these entities in public procurement. What we cannot vote for is an amendment which is contrary to the rules of free competition. We can't accept amendments that don't fit into the law's objectives, either. For example, the provision of premises for private entities to carry out their activities or to introduce an asset for a very specific type of such entities, agricultural cooperatives, as it is not the appropriate framework for their debate. Finally, I shall refer to the red lines which all opposition groups have expressed to me in the Commission, or at the various meetings, concerning transitional and final provisions relating to the reform of the Social Services Act. Red line that is set as an excuse not to support this law, since one cannot appreciate a law of these characteristics only by transitional provisions that gather something to do. We are talking about adapting the autonomous legislation on the authorisation of social services to the basic state legislation, which has already been adapted to Community legislation. But all of the above is done by collecting the specifics. Yes, it eliminates unnecessary regulations, it does not in all cases require the prior authorisation regime, but if it collects the specificities of social services, it collects the protection of people's rights and the guarantee of quality services. In the explanatory statement of the Social Economy Act that is being dealt with, the need to amend this law is clearly justified. In addition, it has been thought to be effective and effective, from a point of view of national policy processing, to take advantage of the processing of this Law of the social economy. In this project, the modification of the authorisation scheme collects the provision of state legislation and the interpretation given in the bilateral committees of the Autonomous Community and if prior authorisation is required for the start of the activity for reasons of public health and directly linked to the guarantee of adequate care and protection of users of social services. In conclusion, the requirement of the authorisation regime subject to the principles of necessity, but also proportionality, is sufficiently motivated legally and specified in its competition, being confined to the applicable legislation and which is to be complied with. Finally, this group will vote against the amendments that the other parliamentary groups hold. Let us maintain the offer of the transaction of Amendment No 14 from BNG, which we would vote for, and of course we will vote in favour of the dictation drawn up by the Commission. Because this law is based on the adoption in Spain in 2011 and follows the European recommendations established from the report of the European Social Council, which points out that despite the importance of the social economy in Europe, 10% of all businesses and 6% of total employment, there is high institutional invisibility, so recognition of its concept, legislation and policies is needed. For this is exactly what the law we're discussing does: to visualize entities, to create a council where the sector will have a direct voice with the Administration and where they can influence public policies designed by the Government of Greece. Nothing else, thank you very much.

They're two different analysis frameworks and, therefore, from those two different analysis frameworks the positions are different. We are going to support the committee's dictates and vote against the amendments that remain, because it is a law that has been analysed by experts on the subject, economists and lawyers, who, if they have made comments and suggestions, are all very far from the amendments that we are discussing today. Because in order to develop the law, the most dynamic sectors of the social economy were consulted in Galicia, represented by cooperative associations in particular, by those represented in the Galician Council of Cooperatives, of which suggestions were also given that were also incorporated into the text. Because it is a law that comes from the will of the economic and social agents of the community and of the Union itself, because it is a law that incorporates important newities, such as the extension of entities in the catalogue – speaking from communities and communities of neighboring mountains in common – including the incorporation into the Social Economy Council of representatives of entities already working in the promotion of them – and speaking from municipalities, speaking from universities; because it incorporates the inclusion of the social economy in the regulated study plans; because it makes these entities think about their active policies, active employment policies and plans that will dynamize the sector; because it creates the figure of the youth cooperative with lower capital requirements for their constitution and lower administrative procedures; and because it also simplifies the procedures for the formation of any cooperative through the implementation of the telematic reporting; because it incorporates something as important as the inclusion of social clauses in the public procurement and – which it seems as important to them to be the voice but the administration is also visible. In the commission we were accused of making electoralism with this law, when I really think that electoralism is being done by you, who, in front of your gallery, thinking about the next elections, cannot reach consensus with the People's Party of Galicia. Because if we can't accept or deal with more amendments, it's not because of a question of electoralism, but because of the reasons, all of them motivated, that I have already explained in my speech in summary and broader terms at the meetings of the panel and the committee and that they fully justify the positioning of this group. Because if we are the first autonomous community to adopt a Law of the Social Economy, it is not because of an electoralist question, but because we have a stable government that meets its commitments and works to improve our society, and does not make empty speeches of content, as is done in the new policy, where we speak of spaces of understanding, lines of consensus, when what we find in this Parliament is red lines and no real commitment to reaching agreements. To conclude, this parliamentary group will vote in favour of the dictates of the committee and of the transaction presented to Amendment No 14 of the Galician Nationalist Block and against the other amendments that are being maintained by the opposition groups, because this law we believe is a very useful law, which will help to improve our economy, to create jobs and also to society in general, because there is a law that has been improved in this Parliament at the meetings of the motion and commission, and because we understand that the amendments that are not accepted do not improve the text, some do not agree with other legislative texts or compliance. Nothing else, thank you very much. [[Applause]] Voting of legislative texts

It's more, when symptoms occur, the Government does what it does is to try to minimize the symptoms and even attribute them to other issues that have nothing to do with reality. Faced with that, the rural councillor, instead of looking at what's happening on our country's farms, simply saying that nothing has to do with the crisis situation in the sector. With more than half a million euros of indebtedness and income that each one of them decreases more because the prices haven't just recalculated. And if you don't resign, allow me to speak, you'd have to run them to the hats of your office. Because the report, endorsed by the ministry, tells us terrible things. The report says that the collection by industries, including by subscribers, has been abandoned. The report says that it is true that for consumers in supermarkets milk has risen, and that today there is only 22% of milk sold at less than 60 cents, but at the same time, the report says that in February the price lowers the average price for livestock 0.6%. It is therefore the theme of the stamp: the crisis in the dairy sector was taken advantage of and the September 23rd agreement to raise milk to consumers but to continue to bring it down to the livestock. A real timo da stampita! And there's a terrible news coming out: 80 gainers of Sarria's cheesery charge 45% of their milk at 14.7 cents, 14.7 cents, ladies and gentlemen! This, if we move it to work, where the minimum wage is EUR 650, is as if we assume with total normality that 40% of the employees of a company are paid EUR 300, less than half of the minimum wage! For here we are paying them with total normality less than half of what it costs to produce a liter of milk, much less than half of what it costs to produce a litre of milk!, and nothing happens, and the Rural Council continues without charging receipt that in the milk sector we have serious problems. But it's just that there's an even more serious problem in terms of the future, which is that we're contracting our sector, we're losing weight. The whole of Europe is increasing milk production, and we are comparatively increasing much less, which means that we are no longer taking up a milk market. And it's very difficult, when you lose a space and others occupy it, one day occupy that space again. We are therefore faced with a terrible phenomenon for the future of the dairy sector in this country, which is, on the one hand, the relocation of milk production. Interestingly, where it grows most is where less natural conditions and less objective conditions have to produce milk, such as Andalusia or Extreme, apart from other clearly surplus European regions. So we have a relocation problem and a low milk cost production problem in Galicia. Finally, the Ministry and the Galician Union – but the most worrying thing is the Galician Union – fifteen days after the European Union's regulation that allows voluntary milk production to be reduced, has not yet said anything in that regard. And, of course, for the Galego Nationalist Block Parliamentary Group, milk reduction should, in any case, be compulsory and at the level of the whole European Union, and, of course, be accompanied by economic incentives. Because, if it is voluntary, what will motivate it is that, if in the Spanish State the production of milk will be reduced voluntarily for six months, it will be the Germans and the Germans, the Germans, who will have to reduce even more milk on their holdings, when, for example, surplus states in the milk production, such as Ireland and Germany, they have already said that they do not intend to reduce even one milligram of milk production; and that will mean that, while we once again sacrifice the Greek holdings and the Greek livestock to cover more those of Germany, Ireland and France, consumers will continue to be those who have to consume the milk surpluses that are produced in Ireland, Germany or France. I mean, once again we're going to be the sinking of milk that remains to the great producing powers of Europe, while forcing our farmers to reduce their production. In this sense, the objective of this proposal is to avoid, or solve, a legal vacuum situation with possible immediate undesirable consequences. One of them is the abandonment of services with the consequent loss of supply of transport on non-responsible routes that materialize in rural areas and in less-income areas. On the other hand, to avoid instability in business viability, especially small and medium-sized enterprises located in rural areas. ParlaMint-ES-GA_2016-04-26-DSPG150.u105	Thank you, President. Good morning to all and to all. The Supreme Administrative Dispute Chamber cancelled last March the Board's decision approving the Grants Modernization Plan. The judgment found an appeal brought by the National Competition Commission against the decision of the Supreme Court of Justice of Galicia which considered this resolution to be law-adjusted. In its appeal, the National Competition Commission argued that the plan involved the automatic extension of all concessions and that it was contrary to the regulation of the European Parliament and of the Council setting the duration of public service contracts, which could not exceed 10 years for bus services. The decision assesses that the extension of concessions is unconditional and gives 10 more years to all existing concessions, irrespective of their initial period, of the period already elapsed or any other circumstance, which violates European legislation; and all these extensions, in exchange for new technological and environmental investments. The adoption in 2010 of the express extension now annulled by the Constitutional was surrounded by controversy in the face of the imminent entry into force of the European regulation of the sector. This law was adopted by the Council and the Galego Transport Committee and the Economic and Social Council for the extension of the deadline without delay. Nor did he refer it to the other councils, as it was mandatory for all draft laws, but especially in this one, which affected the competences of education, such as school transport. The Supreme Court does not question the German law of the year 2009 but questions the resolution of the year 2010. This will bring serious consequences to the road passenger and road passenger transport sector and leaves a critical situation of allegiance to most concessions in Galicia. Here the Government of Galicia is obliged to correct the regulations and apply for contracts that have been illegally extended through the extension system. The famous extension restricted competition because it involved the absolute closure of the market, with disproportionate deadlines, a way of perpetuating traditional operators. This creates long-term oligopolies that are closing other operators the door to entry into the Greek market, which contravened the Community regulation of the year 2007, where it was established that extensions could only be allowed in very specific cases and with a maximum limit of five years. And before that, the PP government quickly promoted the legislative change to circumvent this fact. And three days before this change came into force, the DOG published the law as a matter of urgency, which extended the validity of the concessions for another ten years counted since the due date. The urgent law extended the 143 existing authorizations at that time, in most cases the same company would continue to serve beyond 2020, and the latter will expire by 2029; 44 of these extensions go beyond 2023 – including those that join the cities of Greece with their metropolitan areas –. And besides, a good number of large groups, such as Monbus and Arriva Northwest were the great beneficiaries. Social and qualitative criteria must be established to maintain and increase quality standards for public service obligations with regard to minimum operating conditions, travel and travel rights, the needs of persons with reduced mobility, environmental protection, safety of persons and obligations arising from collective agreements and other rules and agreements relating to workplaces and social protection at the place where the service is provided. The supply controlled under the PDO corresponds to 31% of the total; that is, the amount of mussel coming out of the bats under the support of the Galician Mexillon DOP is 31% of the total, but only 14% is certified, because there is a serious demand problem. The industry is not claiming this certified product. Therefore, the problem is not in the Regulatory Council or in its direction. The responsibility lies, not with the Galician Mexican Regulatory Council, but with a government that defends a part of the processing industry that prefers to work with an uncertified DOP product. That's right, you'd rather work on an uncertified DOP product, but you want to make mention and allusions on the label to a product that is clearly not certified by the protected designation of source. Producers registered by PDO therefore produce 16% of total production devoted to fresh consumption and 54% of total processing. It is clear then that the figures that represent the Mexican DOP in Galicia are quite different from those that tend to be spreading from the German Government. In addition, another of the arguments coming from the Council and the Government is this famous 2003 dictatorship that unties it from the protected designation of origin. A false argument, a loudly false argument, first, because that report is four years earlier to the register of Galician DOP itself in Brussels, which, as you all know, was in 2007, and the adviser and the councillor adhere to a 2003 report. And second, this is roundly false because reality tells us that there's a mexillon in the market that's being protected by the DOP. And we could be talking about 51 million kilograms and 54% of the total if there really existed that demand from the processing industry, because the producers under DOP produced 51 million kilograms of the 96 million conservers in 2014. So what is needed is that the Government no longer understands this as a problem in one part of the sector against another. This must be over by now. Because if we want the whole mussel to guarantee its origin, we have the tool to do so; that's the protected designation of origin. What we should do among all, and above all the Government of Greece, is to strengthen it and not to shoot at it, as they have made different representatives of the Government of Greece in recent times, more specifically the Director General of Fisheries and Aquaculture, Mr Maneiro, as in Commission 8a a few days ago in this Parliament of Galicia said this was a war in the sector. Well, today, with the current production of the battered under the protected designation of origin, we could be certifying 54% of the total mexillone devoted to transformation -- that is, what capacity there is. What is not a commitment from any part of the industry, which is exactly what you are defending, to ensure the origin of a certified product with a protected designation of origin. But what you have to say is that the level of protection of PDO is to ensure that all three processes, both cultivation and processing, are linked to the territory, and with the geographical indication protected only one of these processes has to be linked to the territory. ParlaMint-ES-GA_2016-04-26-DSPG150.u165	To formulate the appearance you have the word Mr. Health Counsellor.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2016-04-26-DSPG150.u166	Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, good afternoon. I compare today in this House to report on the status of treatment of patients with chronic hepatitis C. Hepatitis C is a serious disease that is essentially acquired by contact with the blood of sick people, and of which most of those affected still don't know or suffer it. The first thing I want to convey is a message of tranquillity. Patients in Galicia, especially those of chronic hepatitis C infection, are well treated. We have the best professionals and the latest treatments. It's a matter that I want to address with the utmost respect and consideration of the sick and their families. We owe them a public health system. A different question is the attitude of certain groups that take advantage of this situation to do a petty demagogy. [[Speak words that are not perceived.]] I also want to defend the respect that Sergas and their professionals deserve. At most responsible for the public health system, I have an obligation to defend your prestige, which is being attacked. It is therefore to think of them, of patients, and not to use them and their health as a political tool. First of all, I would like to clarify the texts of requests for representation. The appearance, as the president said, was on his own request and also at the request of the Group of the Socialists of Galicia, with a text which I think is not correct, so I wanted to clarify it, which says "on the prosecution's charge regarding the denial of treatment for hepatitis C patients." In no case has there been denial and I will prove it along and wide this afternoon. The question posed by AGE, BNG and Groupe Mixto says that it reports on the prosecution of alleged reckless homicide and prevarication in relation to the alleged delay in the administration of new hepatitis C drugs by Junta de Galicia. It's the first time I've been summoned here and called the Council of Galicia and not Sergas. There seems to be a clear indication. But it must also be said that what the opposition groups do not in any case appoint is what the judge says, that the judge in this case talks about administrative prevarication, and that's why he quotes two Council members as being investigated. We must deal with this issue rigorously and seriously. The magnitude of this disease is significant in our environment. Many of the patients received transfusions in the past, at times prior to the serological diagnosis of hepatitis C itself, when it was impossible to control the aforementioned transfusions. Let us remember that it is a virus identified on a relatively recent date in 1989. Even there are health professionals who have suffered needle punctures throughout their professional life, and also in case of shared use of syringes in drug abuse and other pathways of infection. In any case, in many cases the origin of the infection has never been clarified, and it is also common to co-infection with other viruses, especially with the AIDS virus. Usually, infection is chronic and has a long period of absence of symptoms but a large proportion of progressive and inexorably liver damage, leading to hepatic cirrhosis, or hepatocarcinoma, and eventually to death. The magnitude of the figures worldwide is more than 170 million hepatitis C patients and one million deaths per year, directly by the virus, by liver cancer caused by hepatitis C virus or by cirrhosis. Obviously, the main objective of treatment is the healing of infection, which has not yet been achieved for a high percentage of patients. Besides, the drugs available until recently had significant side effects. This situation has changed with the emergence of new drugs for the treatment of this pathology. So in recent years we have seen a real outbreak of new drugs that have opened up new and better treatment expectations. The first film was produced in 2012, until then essentially the so-called bitherapy – interferon plus ribavirin – was used. From that year on, it's when it starts to change the situation with new treatments called protease inhibitors, and later on, more treatments, more effective and also safer appeared. Thus, new innovative drugs, such as simeprevir and sofosbuvir, were introduced in 2014, and daclatasvir, ledipasvir, ombitasvir, paritaprevir, ritonavir or dasabuvir also appeared in 2015. Beyond this sequence of hard to pronounce names there is a key fact: these new treatments are proving to be very effective. We must remember, however, that in the early moments, particularly in the early days, there was uncertainty while there was no clear scientific evidence. They suppose better expectations of effectiveness and more security. They were appearing quickly and in a short time, which implies an obvious necessity: to make an orderly introduction of these treatments and, above all, safely for our patients, and this in addition to the principle of prudence and equity that should guide us when using a new treatment. In view of this, I have to say, first and foremost, that Galicia has worked with the Ministry of Health and the rest of the autonomous communities with the ultimate objective of ensuring access to the drugs of these patients, like all other citizens, in terms of equity throughout the National Health System. It can be said that, both in the way of managing access to new medicines and generally in the way of addressing this disease, there is one before and after that determined by the entry into force of the Strategic Plan for the Approach to Hepatitis C in the National Health System, developed by the group of experts designated by the Ministry of Health, Social Services and Equality. Therefore, and for practical purposes, in my exhibition I will clearly distinguish the procedures followed in a period and another. Galicia acted, as I said before, both before and after the Strategic Plan. How do we act before that implantation? Prior to the start of the plan, the Galego Health Service, assuming the responsibilities of prescription and evaluation of pharmacotherapy, constituted in 2010 the Central Autonomous Pharmacy and Therapeutic Commission as the internal advisory body of the Health Council. This commission aimed to monitor the use of pharmacotherapeutic resources in the centres of the Sergas care network, including those related to hepatitis C, and to propose strategies for improving the efficient management of resources, as well as quality, safety and efficiency in their employment, as well as access equity, which is certainly important. I want to highlight a relevant fact: this was not a unique situation in our system; there are also other medicines other than hepatitis C which are also subject to evaluation by the committees of experts in the Galician Health Service; for example, growth hormones, medications for the treatment of multiple sclerosis or others in exceptional situations and those that do not have a therapeutic alternative marketed and funded in the National Health System. Finally, this committee regulates and harmonises the criteria for the use of medicinal products with special health impact so that professionals in Sergas have pharmacotherapeutic guides available for the purpose of ensuring the safety of treatments and access to those medicines once included in the financing and in equal conditions throughout the Galician Health Service. In line with the incorporation into the Spanish market and the introduction into the Spanish market that took place for the first new medicines, called boceprevir and telaprevir, and to ensure, I insist, the safety of patients and homogeneity and equality in access to these medicines in the National Health System, the Ministry of Health, Social Services and Equality, dated February 28, 2012, published the therapeutic positioning report called Criteria and general recommendations for treatment with boceprevir and telaprevir of chronic hepatitis C in infected patients, which was approved by the Interterritorial Council of the National Health System. Then, on 28 June 2012, in the exercise of its functions, the Central Pharmacy and Therapeutic Commission agreed to constitute a specific subcommission for the treatment of hepatitis C because of the importance it had at that time as compared to the volume of patients. On July 12 of that same year, the sub-commission was officially constituted, and had a clear mission: to establish common recommendations for medicines used in hepatitis C and to follow up the treatments established from objective and independent information and within the framework of the recommendations for employment and priority criteria that are established in the field of the National Health System. From this date, July 2012, all applications for initiation of treatment with new protease inhibitors, that is, those then called new treatments, were evaluated anonymously and on a case-by-case basis by the subcommission to verify that the common criteria established within the framework of the National Health System were met; that is, according to scientific evidence, with rigor, effectiveness, safety and equity, always considering the best alternative for our patients. As for their composition, they were part of the health professionals of the hospitals of the Galego Health Service network with knowledge and experience in the management of hepatitis C or the Department of Health Care of the Galego Health Service. I want to insist that the applications were evaluated anonymously, i.e., they were not known either the name or the prescriber of this drug. They were analyzed on a case-by-case basis to verify that the criteria set out in the framework of the National Health System, which I would like to point out once again that they were, and that is very important, to ensure security, homogeneity and equity in access to these new antivirals. This was one of the objectives of counselling carried out by this college committee composed of optional specialists in the treatment of this disease, which evaluated all possible cases susceptible to the treatment of this disease. In this way, it is ensured that patients who meet these technical criteria receive this medication according to their priority. A parliamentary spokesperson, in which I think it was an unfortunate intervention, perhaps to win points for his possible election as candidate of his party to Galician Union, said they could manipulate the data, but the dead are there. Well, I'd say they can manipulate the dead, but the data is here, and that's the data. [[Murmurs.]] The results of the work of this subcommission are there. In a total of 1,150 cases that reached the Commission until the strategic plan came into force, 1,137 were authorised to treat them and 13 did not, because they did not meet the established clinical criteria. By the way, none of these 13 deniers are included among those who are investigating. The most important thing and what should be clear is that all patients who met those criteria had access to the medication they needed. We're talking about a 99% acceptance rate, although, beyond the percentages, all the cases, all of our patients, are important. A proportion of this calibre makes it clear that the argument that they were denied treatment for budgetary reasons is simply false. How do you explain, if not, the deficit that Sergas usually presents at the end of the exercise? Statistically we can figure out what's going to be the approximate number of patients by pathology, but we don't know the new treatments that are going to appear during that year, and logically it usually coincides with that they're more expensive, but of course they're more effective for the patient, so they decide to use them. In 2015, spending on hospital pharmacies grew by 8%, which would be 43% if we included the investment and not spent on new hepatitis C treatments. Thus, except since bad faith, it cannot be fully sustained that a saving was intended not to authorize these drugs, when the fact is that the vast majority have been allowed. This is the context in which the cases of the complaint take place, the eight cases. The fact that the case is now in trial, and that we have an obligation to protect the confidentiality of our patients' data, limits what I can and must say. However, if I can assert that these patients, like all others, were fairly analyzed by the members of the Commission anonymously; like all others, with fairness were evaluated on a case-by-case basis; like all others, with fairness the professionals verified whether they met the common clinical criteria established in the framework of the National Health System; like 99% of the other cases, it was determined that they met the criteria and authorised the treatment to all, regardless of the final outcome. The cases investigated were answered in an average of 18.7 days, and if we excluded one of them, in which successive reassessments were required for possible serious side effects, the average would be 13 days. Important: they were all allowed treatment within a short period of time. It is also important to clarify that these treatments do not cure the disease, but eliminate the infection. Sometimes the problem comes from the damage it has caused in the body for a long time. Ladies and gentlemen, absolutely all cases where the system does not solve a health problem of one of our citizens concern us. Unfortunately, and although medicine has advanced much, very much, we don't always get success in treatments. In this sense, I would like to recall the doctrine established by the Supreme Court, which, by mid-April 29, 2015 states that public authorities are not obliged to produce a result that is not in their hands to ensure, but that their obligation is one of the so-called "media obligations", so that they will have to deploy a set of activities that tend to and are geared towards maintaining, restoring and improving health. No patient has skipped the protocol, there is no documentary falsity. And drugs – which was something else that was valued and commented in different media – could not be acquired with a public contest, because at first they had to be asked for compassionate use or as foreign drugs before they were authorized in Spain, or, being authorised, they were not marketed. And, of course, if you paid the bill of spending that was in 2015 and it's still in 2006. We firmly believe that things have been done with rigour. We trust justice and we have the conviction that this complaint will be resolved favorably. What we deeply regret is that it has been used for pure political purposes. Nothing else, for the moment.

It refers to the development and implementation of this strategic plan at the national level, which was a consensual process, with the prior experience of the autonomous communities, and Galicia, in particular, as I mentioned earlier, had a very important organizational experience that it contributed. But at the Interterritorial Council of the National Health System, held on January 14, 2015, adopted, unanimously from all autonomous communities, the seventeen decided that it was very important to develop this strategic plan at the national level to address hepatitis C disease. Even so, in that interterritorial council, the commission was approved to develop this strategy, led by the prestigious Professor Rodés, and also to update the technical criteria that in each case should be applied according to the therapeutic availability and scientific evidence for the treatment of these patients, taking into account, in addition, as I said, that even then, in that year 2015, it was planned to include more new drugs in the existing therapeutic arsenal. Once in force, since 1 April 2015, the Strategic Plan for the Approach of Hepatitis C in the National Health System, Galicia has diligently assumed this protocol. And as an example, at the time that Galicia was implanted, it was the only community that from day one had at the disposal of professionals a log system that allowed them to know the effectiveness of the new treatments, called the HC Watch. A fundamental element for later control, the registry, and having all the epidemiological data needed to know exactly what activity and effectiveness is, what I'm going to talk about later on in this plan. From that point on, the Hepatitis C Sub-Treatment Sub-Commission no longer assesses requests for treatment, since the clinical criterion of the prescriber remains within the framework set out in the plan, the prescription of the therapeutic alternatives contained therein. Therefore, this subcommission becomes an advisory technical commission for the implementation of the Strategic Plan of the hepatitis C virus. The measures taken by Sergas have already begun before the implementation of the plan, including approval of the inclusion in the pharmacotherapeutic guides of the new medicines available for the treatment of hepatitis C and the updating of the criteria for the use of the medicines already included: access of professionals responsible for the treatment of hepatitis C, the treatment registration platform and effectiveness of hepatitis C, which we call Surveillance, and the meeting with patient associations to collect their concerns or doubts to the draft plan, namely the Galician Association of Hemophilia, Association of Galician Transplanters "Airiños", and the Hepatitis C Affected Platform. The objective of the plan is clear: to reduce the morbidity and mortality caused by hepatitis C virus in the Spanish population, addressing effectively the prevention, diagnosis, treatment and follow-up of patients. So go beyond the mere access to treatments. It includes four strategic lines: one, quantifying the magnitude of the problem, describing the epidemiological characteristics of patients with hepatitis C infection and establishing prevention measures. Two, define the scientific-clinical criteria that allow the appropriate therapeutic strategy to be established, prioritising the use of direct action antivirals for the treatment of hepatitis C in the National Health System and interferon-free therapies. Three, establish coordination mechanisms to properly implement the strategy for the approach of hepatitis C. And, finally, four, encourage progress in the knowledge of the prevention, diagnosis and treatment of hepatitis C in the National Health System, through the action on R+D+i. It should be added that Galicia is part of the Institutional Committee of the Plan to analyse and propose the lines of action to develop in autonomous communities, following the lines of the plan. So, Mrs Solla, this is where the criterion of starting with F4 and F3 was born, the most serious patients, with an essentially criterion, which was what already established the Greek commission, that the evolution of hepatitis C is a slow evolution, and logically the professionals, not the politicians, thought it best to start with those who were more serious, who were closer to a possible death. That's why you used to say it was inappropriate. Treatment was initiated for 2,722 patients, who were 1,859 men and 863 women. These should be added to the 1,137 treated before the plan, resulting in a total of 3,859 treated already 87.41% of the patients assigned to the state plan. 92.98 % carry three groups of treatments, the most commonly used: Harvoni; Viekirax-Exviera (dasabuvir); and another group the sofosbuvir plus daclatasvir. And I think it's a fundamental and very important fact that we're going to add here today, the effectiveness of treatment. Right now, on March 31, assessed after the corresponding weeks have passed, once treatment has been completed, there are 1,149 patients who have already been reassessed and 94.87% of them, i.e., almost 95%, is cured, there is no virus in the blood, which for us is the most important data that can be provided; i.e., there are new treatments and treatments that cure. The patients who would be missing from treatment would be the F0-F1, which at this time progressed, the F2-F4 state of fibrosis and those of 1917 who had a different state of fibrosis, undefined and we are going to reassess. Galicia is now one of the communities that has the highest rates of use of new drugs in relation to its population. In the data from the Ministry of Health, the number of packages consumed in the last year of the new medicines for hepatitis C is 42.35 per 10,000 inhabitants, when the average of the National Health System is 29.27; forty-two versus twenty-nine. Galicia is located 13 points above the average of the National Health System. In 2015 Galicia was the sixth most invested community per capita: 28.3 against others who spent around 10.6. The increase in hospital pharmacy spending in 2015 was 8%, and 43% if we included hepatitis C. In the International Congress – and I think this is a recent, very recent, data from an international congress of hepatology held in Barcelona in recent weeks – it was believed that Spain in 2015 was the sixth country in the world and the first in Europe in the number of patients treated with hepatitis C; absolute numbers, not rates, absolute numbers. Henceforth, there have been reforms and amendments to the law. The great challenges of our education system are, first of all, to achieve universalized and quality public education for all people, regardless of their social, economic, ethnic or personal status, the first major challenge. The second big challenge is to achieve a secular education in which religion is out of public education and reserved for the private sector, which is indeed a challenge. Third challenge: to achieve a quality public education with sufficient resources and staff frameworks, reserving the right of choice of centre – i.e. the right to choose private center – to those people who want and can pay for it; and therefore to stop deriving public resources to private education, with the progressive and gradual elimination of educational concerts to ensure quality public education.
